<div class="ltx_page_main">

<div class="ltx_page_content">

# <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning

<div class="ltx_authors">

<span class="ltx_creator ltx_role_author">
<span class="ltx_personname">Kehua
Feng<sup><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">1,2∗</span></sup>, Keyan
Ding<sup><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">1,2</span></sup> ,
Zhihui Zhu<sup><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">1</span></sup>,
Lei Liang<sup><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">3</span></sup>,
Qiang
Zhang<sup><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">1,2†</span></sup>,
Huajun
Chen<sup><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">1,2</span></sup>  
<sup>1</sup>Zhejiang University   <sup>2</sup>ZJU-Hangzhou Global
Scientific and Technological Innovation Center  
<sup>2</sup>AntGroup </span><span class="ltx_author_notes">Equal
contribution.Corresponding authors.</span></span>

</div>

<div class="ltx_abstract">

###### Abstract

While chain-of-thought (CoT) distillation from advanced large language
models (LLMs) has proven effective in general reasoning tasks, it
struggles in scientific domains where even advanced models often produce
incorrect or superficial reasoning due to high complexity and
specialized knowledge requirements. Directly distilling from such flawed
outputs results in low-quality training data and limits the performance
of smaller student models. To overcome this, we propose
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, an
evolutionary CoT distillation framework. It begins by constructing a
diverse pool of reasoning trajectories from multiple LLM thinkers,
enriches them with automatically retrieved domain knowledge, and
iteratively refines the trajectories using novelty-driven selection,
reflective recombination and mutation. The refinement is guided by a
fitness function that evaluates answer correctness, coherence, and
effective knowledge utilization. This results in a high-quality CoT
dataset tailored for scientific reasoning. We employ this evolved
dataset to fine-tune a compact model, which achieves state-of-the-art
performance on scientific reasoning benchmarks. Our work establishes a
scalable approach to synthesizing high-fidelity scientific reasoning
data from diverse and fallible LLMs.

</div>

<div id="S1" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">1 </span>Introduction

<div id="S1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Recent advances in reasoning large language models (LLMs), such as
DeepSeek-R1 (Guo et al., <a href="#bib.bib10" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>)
and OpenAI-o1/o3 (Jaech et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib15" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>), have demonstrated that
leveraging long and structured chains of thought (CoTs) leads to
remarkable improvements in complex reasoning tasks. CoT distillation
from advanced teacher models has proven effective in general domains (Ye
et al., <a href="#bib.bib34" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>; Hu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib13" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). However, when applied to
scientific domains, even the strongest LLMs frequently generate
erroneous or superficial reasoning paths due to the increasing
complexity and specialization of scientific tasks (Liu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib19" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>; Li et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib18" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). This raises a critical
need for more fine-grained CoT distillation approaches tailored to the
unique requirements of scientific reasoning.

</div>

<div id="S1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Existing work has made significant progress in optimizing CoT
distillation. Some approaches focus on enhancing intra-chain quality in
single-teacher settings by compressing reasoning token length (Wang
et al., <a href="#bib.bib29" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>; Lu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib21" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>) or identifying
intermediate error states (Luo et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib22" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). Others employ
multi-teacher to aggregate diverse reasoning paths and select the most
suitable CoT for each sample (Zhu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib41" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>; Xu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib31" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). While these methods have
enriched the landscape of CoT distillation, they overlook two critical
aspects of scientific reasoning: 1)
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">single-teacher
optimization</span> may introduce bias (Xu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib31" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), and merely pruning
redundant or erroneous steps does not ensure accurate knowledge usage in
the core thought; 2)
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">multi-teacher framework</span>
increases diversity but lacks the flexibility to refine the internal
logic of CoTs at a fine-grained level.

</div>

<div id="S1.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To address these limitations, we propose
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, an
evolutionary CoT distillation framework. Distinct from prior work that
merely selects or transfers a single promising reasoning path per
sample, <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>
performs <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">intra-chain</span>
aggregation, dynamically integrating thoughts from multiple CoTs to
synthesize a single, high-quality chain. Specifically,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> begins by
generating a diverse candidate pool of CoTs using multiple LLMs and
prompting strategies, optionally augmented with external knowledge. Each
candidate is then scored by a fitness function measuring answer
correctness, reasoning length appropriateness, and knowledge usage
accuracy. Rather than greedily picking the top candidates,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> employs a
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">novelty-driven selection</span>
mechanism that jointly rewards quality and behavioral diversity.
Finally, reflective recombination and mutation operators integrate or
revise reasoning steps across candidates to produce improved offspring
chains. Iterating this evaluation–selection–variation–update loop yields
a compact yet high-fidelity set of evolved CoTs for downstream training.

</div>

<div id="S1.p4" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Our <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">contributions</span> can be
summarized as follows:

- <span id="S1.I1.i1"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S1.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  We introduce the first intra-chain multi-trajectory aggregation
  framework for CoT distillation, enabling fine-grained integration of
  reasoning steps within a single chain.

  </div>
- <span id="S1.I1.i2"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S1.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  We propose an evolutionary CoT distillation approach that integrates
  novelty-driven selection, reflective recombination, and mutation to
  iteratively refine reasoning trajectories.

  </div>
- <span id="S1.I1.i3"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S1.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  We construct an evolved CoT dataset and demonstrate that our method
  significantly improves the performance of student models on scientific
  reasoning tasks.

  </div>

</div>

<figure id="S1.F1" class="ltx_figure">
<img src="/html/2510.13166/assets/x1.png" id="S1.F1.g1"
class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_square" width="830"

</figure>

</div>

<div id="S2" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">2 </span>Related Work

<div id="S2.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Long CoT Distillation

<div id="S2.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Long CoTs have been shown to significantly enhance LLMs’ reasoning
ability, as evidenced by models like DeepSeek-R1 (Guo et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib10" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>) and OpenAI-o1/o3 (Jaech
et al., <a href="#bib.bib15" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>). Distilling such
trajectories from strong teacher models into smaller ones has become a
common strategy to boost reasoning efficiency under limited
computation (Ye et al., <a href="#bib.bib34" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>;
Hu et al., <a href="#bib.bib13" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). To improve
quality, prior work has explored intra-chain optimization, *e.g*.,
compressing reasoning token length or pruning erroneous steps (Wang
et al., <a href="#bib.bib29" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>; Luo et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib22" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), and inter-chain (*i.e*.,
multi-teacher) strategies, *e.g*., aggregating multiple reasoning paths
from different teachers or prompting paradigms (Chain-of-Thought (Wei
et al., <a href="#bib.bib30" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>),
Tree-of-Thought (Yao et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib33" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>), or
Program-of-Thought (Chen et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib6" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>)) (Zhu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib41" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>; Xu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib31" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>; Lei et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib17" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). Despite these advances,
existing methods remain limited in scientific domains, where ensuring
both factual accuracy and logical rigor is critical. To this end, we
introduce <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>,
which applies an evolutionary, fine-grained intra-chain aggregation
strategy to combine and refine reasoning steps across CoTs, yielding
more reliable and domain-accurate scientific reasoning trajectories.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S2.SS0.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Reasoning in Scientific Domains

<div id="S2.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

With the rapid progress of LLMs in mathematics and code, recent efforts
have turned to evaluating their capabilities in scientific domains.
Benchmarking efforts have shifted focus from high school-level exams
such as MMLU (Hendrycks et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib11" class="ltx_ref">2020</a>), CEval (Huang et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib14" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>), and AGIEval (Zhong
et al., <a href="#bib.bib40" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>), to more advanced
scientific tests including GPQA (Rein et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib26" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>), SciEval (Sun et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib27" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>), and SciKnowEval (Feng
et al., <a href="#bib.bib8" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>), and further to
domain-intensive tasks such as BioMaze (Zhao et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib36" class="ltx_ref">2025a</a>), BioProBench (Liu
et al., <a href="#bib.bib19" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), and
ChemCoTBench (Li et al., <a href="#bib.bib18" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>),
which emphasize reasoning over memorization, covering areas from
biological protocols to molecular design. Moreover, developing
specialized scientific LLMs to tackle complex downstream tasks has
emerged as a promising direction (Zhao et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib37" class="ltx_ref">2025b</a>; Bai et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib1" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>). In this paper, we aim to
provide a generalizable CoT distillation framework that generates
high-quality reasoning trajectories adaptable to diverse scientific
applications.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">3 </span><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>: Evolutionary CoT Distillation

<div id="S3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

In this section, we present
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, a novel
evolutionary framework tailored for CoT distillation in scientific
reasoning tasks. Let
$`\mathcal{D}_{\text{ori}}=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}`$ denote the
original training dataset without CoT. For each $`x_{i}`$, the goal of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> is to generate
high-fidelity, compact CoTs for downstream training by leveraging a
diverse <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">candidate pool</span>
$`\mathcal{P}=\{t_{1},t_{2},\dots,t_{n}\}`$ produced from a collection
of LLM thinkers $`\mathcal{L}`$. Through iterative refinement guided by
carefully crafted reward signals,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> evolves these
candidates into more accurate and domain-reliable reasoning chains.

</div>

<div id="S3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Following the principle of genetic algorithm,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> consists of
four core modules: 1) multi-thinker CoT initialization (Section
<a href="#S3.SS1" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.1 Multi-thinker CoT Initialization ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.1</span></a>), 2) novelty-driven
candidate selection (Section <a href="#S3.SS3" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.3 Novelty-Driven Candidate Selection ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.3</span></a>), 3) reflective CoT
recombination and mutation (Section <a href="#S3.SS4" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.4 Reflective CoT Recombination and Mutation ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.4</span></a>), and 4) fitness function
definition (Section <a href="#S3.SS2" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.2 Fitness Function ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>). We summarize the
optimization loop of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> in Section
<a href="#S3.SS5" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.5 CoT-Evo Optimization Loop ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.5</span></a>. Figure
<a href="#S1.F1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 1 ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> provides an overview of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, and the full
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> algorithm is
formalized in Algorithm <a href="#alg1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Algorithm 1 ‣ Appendix A1 Algorithm of CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="S3.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.1 </span>Multi-thinker CoT Initialization

<div id="S3.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

A key to the success of evolutionary algorithms lies in constructing an
initial <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">candidate pool</span>
$`\mathcal{P}`$ that is both diverse and promising. To this end, we
employ a two-stage approach.

</div>

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### CoT Generation

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We first assemble a collection of LLM thinkers
$`\mathcal{L}=\{l_{1},l_{2},\dots,l_{m}\}`$, where each $`l_{i}`$ may
represent: 1) reasoning-based LLMs from different model families or
scales, *e.g*., DeepSeek-R1 (Guo et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib10" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), Qwen3-235B-A22B (Yang
et al., <a href="#bib.bib32" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), and Qwen3-32B;
or 2) instruction-tuned LLMs with varying prompting strategies, *e.g*.,
Tree-of-Thought (Yao et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib33" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>), Chain-of-Thought (Wei
et al., <a href="#bib.bib30" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>), and backward
reasoning. Formally, for query $`x\in\mathcal{D}_{\text{ori}}`$, each
trajectory $`t_{i}\in\mathcal{P}^{G}`$ is generated by

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
t_{i}=l_{i}(x),\quad i=1,2,\dots,m.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(1)</span> |

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Knowledge Augmentation

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Considering the high specialization of scientific domains, a model’s
internal knowledge alone is often insufficient. Therefore, we collect
additional knowledge required for each query $`x`$ using a prompt-based
automated approach. Specifically, for each QA pair
$`(x,y)\in\mathcal{D}_{\text{ori}}`$, we prompt an advanced proprietary
model $`\Theta`$ to perform reflective reasoning from $`y`$ to identify
the knowledge necessary for solving $`x`$, and then abstract it into
general, context-independent snippets $`\mathcal{K}_{x}`$. We then
randomly select a subset of thinkers, and provide each chosen thinker
$`l_{j}`$ with $`\mathcal{K}_{x}`$ as additional context, resulting in a
knowledge-enhanced CoT $`t_{j}\in\mathcal{P}^{K}`$ represented as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
t_{j}=l_{j}(x,\mathcal{K}_{x}),\quad j=1,2,\dots,n-m.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(2)</span> |

</div>

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This approach results in an initial candidate pool
$`\mathcal{P}=\mathcal{P}^{G}\cup\mathcal{P}^{K}`$ that maximizes both
cognitive difficulty (Cai et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib3" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>) and strategic diversity,
simulating a broad coverage of the solution space.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.2 </span>Fitness Function

<div id="S3.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We evaluate each candidate trajectory $`t`$ using a composite fitness
score that captures 1) answer correctness, 2) reasoning-length
appropriateness, and 3) correctness of knowledge usage.

</div>

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Exact Match

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We use a task-specific external script to check whether the candidate’s
final answer exactly matches the ground truth. The exact match score is
binary:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
s_{\text{EM}}=\begin{cases}1\quad\text{if exact match,}\\[3.0pt]
0\quad\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(3)</span> |

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Length Appropriateness

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We compute token lengths from scientific reasoning datasets (*e.g*.,
Llama-Nemotron-Science (Bercovich et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib2" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>)) and set the 15% and 85%
percentiles as lower and upper bounds, since responses shorter than the
15% threshold often miss key knowledge, while those beyond the 85%
threshold tend to be verbose. Thus, the length appropriateness score is
defined as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
s_{\text{LEN}}=\begin{cases}0.0&\text{if $\text{len}(t)$}<\text{15\% percentile,}\\
0.5&\text{if $\text{len}(t)$}>\text{85\% percentile,}\\
1.0&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(4)</span> |

This encourages concise reasoning while avoiding under-explained
outputs.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Knowledge Usage Correctness

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We employ an LLM-as-a-Judge (Zheng et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib38" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>) evaluator to assess the
accuracy of the knowledge applied in the core thought of $`t`$. Given
the reference knowledge $`\mathcal{K}_{x}`$ and the CoT $`t`$, the judge
assigns a categorical score

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
s_{\text{KNOW}}=\text{Judge}(\mathcal{K}_{x},t),\quad s_{\text{KNOW}}\in\{1,2,3,4,5\}.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(5)</span> |

</div>

<div id="S3.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

The final fitness function of $`t`$ combines the three components with
weighted contributions:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
\mathcal{R}(t)=s_{\text{EM}}+\lambda_{1}s_{\text{LEN}}+\lambda_{2}s_{\text{KNOW}},
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(6)</span> |

where $`\lambda_{1}`$ and $`\lambda_{2}`$ control the relative
importance of length and knowledge usage.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.3 </span>Novelty-Driven Candidate Selection

<div id="S3.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

While the initial pool $`\mathcal{P}`$ obtained in
Section <a href="#S3.SS1" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.1 Multi-thinker CoT Initialization ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.1</span></a> offers substantial diversity
in reasoning trajectories, selecting candidates solely based on the
highest fitness scores can lead to premature convergence toward a narrow
set of CoTs. To mitigate this,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> employs a
*novelty-driven selection* mechanism, inspired by Novelty Search with
Local Competition (NSLC) (Lehman & Stanley,
<a href="#bib.bib16" class="ltx_ref">2011</a>), which simultaneously
promotes two complementary objectives: (i) encouraging distinct
reasoning patterns, and (ii) rewarding local quality improvements.

</div>

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Behavioral Embedding

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We first embed each CoT $`t\in\mathcal{P}`$ into a $`d`$-dimensional
*behavioral space* through a mapping
$`b:\mathcal{P}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ capturing its structural or
semantic reasoning features:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
\mathbf{z}_{t}=b(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(7)</span> |

In practice, we instantiate $`b(\cdot)`$ using
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Qwen3-Embedding-8B</span> (Zhang
et al., <a href="#bib.bib35" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>).

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Novelty Score

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To quantify distinctiveness, we compute trajectory $`t`$’s average
distance to its $`k`$ nearest neighbors $`\mathcal{N}_{k}(t)`$ in
behavioral space:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
N(t)=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{k}(t)}\bigl\|\mathbf{z}_{t}-\mathbf{z}_{t^{\prime}}\bigr\|_{2}.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(8)</span> |

Larger $`N(t)`$ values correspond to more differentiated reasoning
styles.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Local Competition Score

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Given a fitness function $`\mathcal{R}(t)`$ (defined in
Section <a href="#S3.SS2" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.2 Fitness Function ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>) that assesses the
correctness and quality of a trajectory $`t`$, we evaluate $`t`$’s
relative improvement over its neighbors:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
L(t)=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{N}_{k}(t)}\bigl(\mathcal{R}(t)-\mathcal{R}(t^{\prime})\bigr)_{+},\quad(a)_{+}=\max(a,0).
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(9)</span> |

Here, $`L(t)`$ rewards local superiority within the same behavioral
region.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px4" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Pareto-Based Selection

<div id="S3.SS3.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We treat each $`(N(t),L(t))`$ pair as a bi-objective performance vector
$`\mathbf{g}(t)=(N(t),L(t))`$. Define a (strict) Pareto-dominance
relation $`\mathbf{g}(t^{\prime})\succ\mathbf{g}(t)`$, if
$`N(t^{\prime})\geq\!N(t)\wedge L(t^{\prime})\geq L(t)`$ with at least
one strict inequality. The Pareto front is then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
\mathcal{F}_{t}=\{\,t\in\mathcal{P}\mid\nexists\,t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}\ \text{s.t.}\ \mathbf{g}(t^{\prime})\succ\mathbf{g}(t)\,\}.
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(10)</span> |

From $`\mathcal{F}_{t}`$, parent candidates are sampled with probability

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
p(t)=\frac{L(t)+\varepsilon}{\sum\limits_{t^{\prime}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}}\bigl(L(t^{\prime})+\varepsilon\bigr)},\quad\varepsilon>0,
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(11)</span> |

where the parameter $`\varepsilon`$ ensures numerical stability and
slightly favors trajectories with higher local performance while
maintaining diversity.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS4" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.4 </span>Reflective CoT Recombination and Mutation

<div id="S3.SS4.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To further evolve reasoning trajectories,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> adopts a
*reflective* approach for recombination and mutation, enabling models to
incorporate useful strategies from peers or revise their own reasoning
patterns.

</div>

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### CoT Recombination.

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Recombination is triggered only if $`t_{o}`$ yields an incorrect final
answer (*i.e*., $`s_{\text{EM}}(t_{o})=0`$). Unlike standard genetic
algorithms, our crossover operates on a single *target* CoT $`t_{o}`$
with guidance from a *strategy provider* $`t_{p}`$. Let
$`\mathcal{C}_{r}`$ denote the recombiner (defaulting to
$`l_{o}\in\mathcal{L}`$ that produced $`t_{o}`$).

</div>

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Step 1: Identify binding
point.</span> We decompose $`t_{p}`$ into distinct *thoughts* using
transition keywords (Chai et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib4" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>) (see
Appendix <a href="#A6" class="ltx_ref"
title="Appendix A6 Transition Keywords ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A6</span></a> for details), then apply
$`\mathcal{C}_{r}`$ to select the endpoint of the last reasonable
thought as the binding point $`\mathcal{B}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{C}_{r}(t_{p},\text{transition keywords}).
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(12)</span> |

</div>

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px1.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Step 2: Cross-chain
recombination.</span> We employ $`\mathcal{C}_{r}`$ to extract unique
steps and knowledge from $`t_{p}`$ absent in $`t_{o}`$, denoted by
$`\mathcal{I}`$, and let $`\mathcal{C}_{r}`$ generate a new CoT
$`t^{\prime}`$ conditioned on the prefix $`t_{o}[:\mathcal{B}]`$ and
$`\mathcal{I}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
t^{\prime}=t_{o}[:\mathcal{B}]\ +\ \mathcal{C}_{r}\big(t_{o}[:\mathcal{B}],\mathcal{I}\big).
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(13)</span> |

Owing to the limited instruction-following capability of currently
reasoning models, if $`\mathcal{C}_{r}`$ is a reasoning model, we
explicitly insert guiding text immediately after $`\mathcal{C}_{r}`$’s
initial “<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">\<think\></span>”
token, including: 1) “I can receive externally provided information
through
“<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">\<info\>\</info\></span>”,
and 2) “Upon receiving information, I should switch to a new thought and
verify and use that information.”

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Reflective Mutation.

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Mutation aims to alter $`t_{o}`$’s strategy, logic, or expression. Given
$`t_{o}`$ and mutation operator $`\mathcal{M}_{u}`$ (defaulting to
$`l_{o}`$), we generate new variants via three modes:

</div>

<div id="S3.SS4.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

*(1) Additive mutation*: enrich logical detail, explanations, and domain
knowledge:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
t^{\prime}_{(a)}=\mathcal{M}_{u}(t_{o},\text{Add});
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(14)</span> |

*(2) Deletive mutation*: prune redundancy, unproductive exploration, or
extraneous knowledge:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
t^{\prime}_{(d)}=\mathcal{M}_{u}(t_{o},\text{Delete});
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(15)</span> |

*(3) Innovative mutation:* diagnoses erroneous logic in $`t_{o}`$ using
the correct answer $`y`$, then generates a new trajectory that attempts
to avoid the identified mistakes:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | 
``` math
t^{\prime}_{(c)}=\mathcal{M}_{u}\big(\mathcal{M}_{u}(t_{o},\text{Innovate}),\text{Delete}\big).
``` |  | <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_equation ltx_align_right">(16)</span> |

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S3.SS5" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.5 </span><span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> Optimization Loop

<div id="S3.SS5.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Putting the above modules together,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> proceeds in an
iterative evolutionary loop that progressively refines the candidate
pool, which is similar to the standard genetic algorithm Holland
(<a href="#bib.bib12" class="ltx_ref">1992</a>). At each generation, the
current pool $`\mathcal{P}`$ produced by the multi-thinker
initialization (Section <a href="#S3.SS1" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.1 Multi-thinker CoT Initialization ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.1</span></a>) is first evaluated by the
fitness function $`\mathcal{R}(t)`$
(Section <a href="#S3.SS2" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.2 Fitness Function ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>) to obtain the quality
scores of all trajectories. Rather than greedily selecting only the
top-scoring CoTs, we apply the novelty-driven selection mechanism
(Section <a href="#S3.SS3" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.3 Novelty-Driven Candidate Selection ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.3</span></a>) to choose a set of parent
trajectories that are both diverse in reasoning behavior and locally
competitive. These parents are then passed to the reflective
recombination and mutation operators
(Section <a href="#S3.SS4" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.4 Reflective CoT Recombination and Mutation ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.4</span></a>) to generate improved
offspring trajectories, which inherit useful strategies or undergo
targeted revisions of their reasoning steps. The resulting offspring are
merged with the previous pool, with the lowest-fitness trajectories
removed to maintain the population size $`n_{\text{pop}}`$, forming a
new candidate set for the next iteration of the
evaluation–selection–variation cycle. This iterative process continues
until convergence or a predefined budget $`B`$, yielding an evolved CoT
dataset
$`\mathcal{D}_{\text{evo}}=\{(x_{i},t_{i}^{\star},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{N}`$
with $`t_{i}^{\star}=\arg\max_{t\in\mathcal{P}_{x_{i}}}\mathcal{R}(t)`$
for downstream model training.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S4" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">4 </span>Experiments

<div id="S4.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">4.1 </span>Implementation of <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>

<div id="S4.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Our pool of LLM thinkers includes Qwen3-32B-think (Yang et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib32" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), DeepSeek-R1 (Guo et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib10" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), and
Qwen3-235B-A22B-think (Yang et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib32" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>), together with three
prompting strategies based on Llama4-Scout-17B-16E (Meta,
<a href="#bib.bib23" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>):
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">backward reasoning</span> (Chen
et al., <a href="#bib.bib5" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>),
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Chain-of-Thought</span> (Wei
et al., <a href="#bib.bib30" class="ltx_ref">2022</a>), and
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Tree-of-Thought</span> (Yao
et al., <a href="#bib.bib33" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>). For knowledge
augmentation in Section <a href="#S3.SS1" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.1 Multi-thinker CoT Initialization ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.1</span></a>, we employ GPT-5-mini to
generate the knowledge snippets $`\mathcal{K}_{x}`$. In deploying the
fitness function, we set $`\lambda_{1}=0.3`$ and $`\lambda_{2}=0.1`$.
The optimization loop of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> uses a fixed
population size of $`n_{\text{pop}}=6`$ per epoch with a computation
budget of $`B=5`$ epochs. During novelty-driven candidate selection, we
set the $`k`$-nearest neighbors parameter to $`k=2`$, and from the
resulting Pareto front $`\mathcal{F}_{t}`$, parent trajectories are
randomly sampled (three per iteration by default) for recombination and
mutation. Consequently, each query can theoretically yield up to
$`6+3\times 5=21`$ distinct CoTs.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We apply <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> to
three popular open-source LLMs:
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Qwen3-8B</span> (Yang et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib32" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>),
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct</span> (Team,
<a href="#bib.bib28" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>), and
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Llama3.1-8B-Instruct</span> (Grattafiori
et al., <a href="#bib.bib9" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>).

</div>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">4.2 </span>Experimental Setup

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS1" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">4.2.1 </span>Datasets

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To comprehensively evaluate
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, we focus on
two representative scientific reasoning datasets, ChemCoTDataset and
BioProBench, that capture the challenges of molecular editing and
optimization, and experimental protocol design.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">ChemCoTDataset</span> (Li et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib18" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>): This dataset contains
14K samples tailored for chemical reasoning. Complex tasks (*e.g*.,
molecular design) are decomposed into modular, verifiable operations
(*e.g*., substructure addition, deletion, or replacement), ensuring that
the reasoning process is transparent and step-wise. It encompasses four
tasks, including molecular understanding (Und.), molecular editing
(Edit), reaction prediction (Reaction), and molecular optimization
(Opt.). We use ChemCoTDataset for training data construction and
evaluate on its associated benchmark, ChemCoTBench.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS1.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">BioProBench</span> (Liu et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib19" class="ltx_ref">2025</a>): This benchmark assesses
LLMs’ ability to reason over biological experimental protocols. It
transforms 27K real-world protocols into 306K training samples and 3.6K
test samples covering protocol QA (PQA), step ordering (ORD), and error
correction (ERR) tasks. For computational efficiency, we use a 20K
subset of the training QA pairs for CoT construction while retaining the
full test set for evaluation.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS2" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">4.2.2 </span>Baselines

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

For a comprehensive and fair comparison, we compare
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> with the
following baselines:

</div>

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

- <span id="S4.I1.i1"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">LLM Thinkers (Teachers).</span>
  We also directly evaluate the six LLM thinkers used in our
  initialization stage, including DeepSeek-R1, Qwen3-235B-A22B,
  Qwen3-32B, and Llama4-Scout-17B-16E with three prompting strategies.
  These serve as the upper bound references for evaluating the
  distillation quality of
  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.

  </div>
- <span id="S4.I1.i2"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Base Models.</span> We directly
  evaluate the original instruct or reasoning LLMs without additional
  fine-tuning, serving as a reference to measure the overall gain of
  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.

  </div>
- <span id="S4.I1.i3"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Single Teacher (ST).</span> For
  each LLM thinker, we apply the fitness function
  (Section <a href="#S3.SS2" class="ltx_ref"
  title="3.2 Fitness Function ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
  class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>) to filter its CoTs and
  retain only those with exact-match correct answers
  ($`s_{\text{EM}}=1`$). We repeat this process for all thinkers and
  report the performance of the best individual teacher.

  </div>
- <span id="S4.I1.i4"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I1.i4.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Multi Teacher (MT).</span> For
  each query, we aggregate CoTs from all thinkers and directly select
  the correct trajectory with the highest fitness score. This
  representative CoT is used for training, thereby capturing diversity
  across teachers. If no correct CoT exists, the query is discarded.

  </div>
- <span id="S4.I1.i5"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="S4.I1.i5.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Best-of-K (BoK).</span> For each
  LLM thinker, we sample $`K=21`$ trajectories (matching the maximum
  number produced by
  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> for a single
  query), apply rejection sampling based on the fitness function, and
  select the correct CoT with the highest score.

  </div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS3" class="section ltx_subsubsection">

#### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsubsection">4.2.3 </span>Evaluation Metrics

<div id="S4.SS2.SSS3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

For <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">BioProBench</span>, we evaluate
the protocol QA (PQA) task using Accuracy (Acc), the step ordering (ORD)
task using Exact Match (EM), and the error correction (ERR) task using
both Acc and F1 score. For
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">ChemCoTBench</span>, we adopt
task-specific evaluation metrics. In molecular understanding (Und.), we
apply mean absolute error (MAE) for functional group counting, Tanimoto
molecule similarity (TMS) for Murcko scaffold extraction, and accuracy
for the remaining subtasks. Molecular editing (Edit) is evaluated with
accuracy, reaction prediction (Reaction) is assessed by fingerprint
similarity (FTS) with reference molecules, and molecular optimization
(Opt.) is measured by success rate (SR), indicating the generated
molecules that achieve the desired property improvement.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">4.3 </span>Main Results

<div id="S4.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_upright">
delivers consistent performance gains over distillation
baselines.</span></span> Table <a href="#S4.T1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 1 ‣ 4.2.1 Datasets ‣ 4.2 Experimental Setup ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> reports the performance of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> compared with
existing distillation methods. Our approach consistently outperforms
both single-teacher (ST) and multi-teacher (MT) baselines across the two
benchmarks. On BioProBench,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> yields relative
gains of 12.6% over ST and 8.4% over MT, while on ChemCoTBench the
improvements reach 27.0% and 19.3%, respectively. Notably, on several
tasks, <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> even
rivals the performance of the LLM teachers themselves. Such gains stem
from <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> not only
filtering out erroneous or redundant reasoning from teachers but also
integrating complementary reasoning strategies across multiple thinkers,
yielding distilled CoTs that are often more accurate and task-aligned
than the raw outputs of the teacher LLMs. These results highlight the
overall advantage of evolutionary CoT distillation in producing stronger
student models.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">The performance gains of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> go beyond the
effect of increased sampling.</span> As described in
Section <a href="#S4.SS1" class="ltx_ref"
title="4.1 Implementation of CoT-Evo ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4.1</span></a>,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> can
theoretically generate up to 21 candidate CoTs per query. To examine
whether its superior performance merely stems from this enlarged
sampling budget, we compare
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> with the
Best-of-K (BoK) baseline, where each teacher independently samples
$`K=21`$ CoTs and selects the best one according to the fitness
function. Results in Table <a href="#S4.T1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 1 ‣ 4.2.1 Datasets ‣ 4.2 Experimental Setup ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> demonstrate that
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> substantially
outperforms BoK, confirming that the improvements arise from
higher-quality CoT evolution rather than brute-force data scaling.

</div>

<figure id="S4.T2" class="ltx_table ltx_align_floatright">

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table 2:
</span>Comparison of data utilization (Pass), reasoning quality
(Quality) and win rate (WR) across distillation baselines and <span
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="S4.SS3.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_upright">
improves both data utilization and reasoning quality.</span></span> To
validate the source of these improvements, we analyze how
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> compares with
baseline approaches in terms of usable data and CoT quality. For
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">data utilization</span>, we
measure the proportion of distilled trajectories that are correct and
usable. For <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">reasoning
quality</span>, we employ GPT-5 as a judge to score CoTs across five
dimensions: knowledge diversity, knowledge accuracy, reasoning
diversity, logical coherence, and step redundancy. In addition, we
conduct pairwise comparisons between
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> and baseline
CoTs, reporting win rates to capture relative quality. As shown in
Table <a href="#S4.T2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 2 ‣ 4.3 Main Results ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>,
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> maintains over
70% usable data while producing CoTs that are more knowledge-rich and
logically compact. Furthermore, even the strongest baseline achieves a
win rate below 40%, demonstrating that its gains come not just from more
data, but from better data.

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">4.4 </span>Ablation Study

<figure id="S4.T3" class="ltx_table ltx_align_floatright">

on recombination and mutation.</figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Necessity of Recombination and Mutation.

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To evaluate the effectiveness of the core modules for CoT optimization,
we conduct ablation experiments by testing 1)
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> without
recombination, and 2)
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> without
mutation on BioProBench. As shown in
Table <a href="#S4.T3" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 3 ‣ 4.4 Ablation Study ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, removing either module leads
to a noticeable performance drop. However, the roles of recombination
and mutation differ in important ways. Recombination primarily enhances
the diversity of reasoning trajectories and promotes better utilization
of knowledge; thus, removing this module degrades data quality and in
turn weakens the performance of the student model. Mutation, on the
other hand, is designed to correct errors and reduce redundant reasoning
within CoTs. Without this module, the convergence of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> is hindered,
resulting in fewer usable correct CoTs and consequently a more
significant decline in student models’ performance.

</div>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Effectiveness of Selection Strategy.

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To assess the effectiveness of our proposed novelty-driven selection
(NDS) strategy, we compare it with two straightforward baselines: 1)
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">greedy selection</span>, which
always chooses the candidates with the highest fitness score
$`\mathcal{R}`$; and 2) <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">random
selection</span>, which samples candidates uniformly at random. Average
Pass@1 results on BioProBench are shown in
Figure <a href="#S4.F2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 2 ‣ 4.3 Main Results ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> (left). Across all three base
models, NDS consistently surpasses the baselines. This trend can be
explained by the evolution of fitness scores
(Figure <a href="#S4.F2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 2 ‣ 4.3 Main Results ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>, right): random selection
yields steady but slow improvements; greedy selection saturates within
the first few epochs and often converges to local optima; in contrast,
NDS drives faster and more stable gains, producing progressively
higher-quality CoTs. Importantly, the budget of $`B=5`$ remains well
below the saturation point of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, suggesting
further room for improvement.

</div>

<figure id="S4.F3.fig1"
class="ltx_figure ltx_minipage ltx_align_center ltx_align_middle ltx_align_floatright"
style="width:433.6pt;">
<div class="ltx_flex_figure">
<div class="ltx_flex_cell ltx_flex_size_1">
<div

height="710" alt="Refer to caption" />
</div>
</div>
<div class="ltx_flex_break">

</div>

<div

of budget (left) and population size (right) on <span
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>’s distillation
performance.</figcaption>
</figure>

</div>

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Algorithm Scalability

<div id="S4.SS4.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We aim to validate whether the scale of our algorithm is a key factor
affecting distillation performance. Specifically,
Figure <a href="#S4.F3.fig1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 3 ‣ Effectiveness of Selection Strategy. ‣ 4.4 Ablation Study ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> investigates two major
hyperparameters controlling the scale of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>: the iteration
budget $`B`$ and the population size $`n_{\text{pop}}`$. As shown in
Figure <a href="#S4.F3.fig1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 3 ‣ Effectiveness of Selection Strategy. ‣ 4.4 Ablation Study ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> (left), increasing the
iteration budget initially leads to clear performance gains
($`B\leq 3`$), but these improvements gradually level off in later
stages (in contrast to Figure  <a href="#S4.F2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 2 ‣ 4.3 Main Results ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>). This may be because we
always select the highest-fitness CoTs from the candidate pool; after
several epochs, correct and high-fitness CoTs are already obtained,
reducing the marginal benefit of further iterations. A similar trend can
be observed in the population-size curves (Figure
<a href="#S4.F3.fig1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Figure 3 ‣ Effectiveness of Selection Strategy. ‣ 4.4 Ablation Study ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, right). Empirically, when
$`n_{\text{pop}}<5`$, enlarging the population size enhances the
diversity of the initial candidate pool at each iteration, which
facilitates more effective recombination. Overall, increasing the scale
consistently improves
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>’s performance,
and near-optimal gains can be achieved under relatively modest budgets.

</div>

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="S5" class="section ltx_section">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">5 </span>Conclusion

<div id="S5.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

In this paper, we introduced
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, an
evolutionary framework for chain-of-thought (CoT) distillation that goes
beyond prior methods by performing intra-chain aggregation, integrating
reasoning steps from multiple CoTs through an iterative
evaluation–selection–variation–update process. This design yields
distilled CoTs that are more accurate, rigorous, and diverse while
remaining compact and efficient for training smaller scientific models.
Extensive experiments show that
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> consistently
outperforms existing distillation approaches. Looking forward, we aim to
evaluate its generalization across broader scientific domains, and to
enhance the knowledge augmentation stage by incorporating external
resources such as domain databases or structured knowledge graphs,
making <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> more
scalable, knowledge-grounded, and practical for real-world scientific
applications.

</div>

</div>

<div id="Sx1" class="section ltx_section">

## Limitations

<div id="Sx1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

While <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>
demonstrates strong performance in scientific reasoning distillation, it
is not without limitations. First, leveraging multiple LLM thinkers
introduces substantial computational and time costs, as generating and
refining diverse trajectories requires repeated sampling and evaluation.
This cost may hinder scalability to very large datasets or
resource-constrained environments. Second, our evolutionary process
currently relies on LLM-based reward signals to evaluate reasoning
quality; while effective, these signals may still carry biases, and
future work could explore integrating more verifiable reward signals
(*e.g*., rule-based) to further enhance robustness.

</div>

</div>

<div id="Sx2" class="section ltx_section">

## Ethics Statement

<div id="Sx2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This research aims to enhance the reliability of AI for scientific
reasoning. Our experiments are conducted exclusively on public
scientific benchmarks, which contain no sensitive or personally
identifiable information. While we utilize pre-trained LLMs, our
framework is designed to filter and refine their outputs to improve
factual accuracy and logical rigor. We have made every effort to
mitigate potential risks, such as the generation of incorrect scientific
content, and therefore foresee no significant ethical issues arising
from this work.

</div>

</div>

<div id="Sx3" class="section ltx_section">

## Reproducibility Statement

<div id="Sx3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

We are committed to the full reproducibility of our research. We will
release our source code for the
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> framework,
fine-tuning scripts, and evaluation protocols. The paper provides
comprehensive details on the public datasets, models, and
hyperparameters used. Furthermore, all prompts required to replicate our
evolutionary process are documented in the appendix. These resources
will enable the community to verify our findings and build upon our
work.

</div>

</div>

distillation methods (ST, MT, BoK) and original LLM teachers across
BioProBench and ChemCoTBench. Best results are marked in <span
class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">bold</span>, and second-best results are
<span
class="ltx_text ltx_framed ltx_framed_underline">underlined</span>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table">Table A2:
</span>Comparison of data utilization (Pass), reasoning quality
(Quality) and win rate (WR) across distillation baselines and <span
class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.</figcaption>
</figure>

<div id="A2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

In Section 4, we reported the performance of the
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Single Teacher</span> (ST)
baseline on BioProBench and ChemCoTBench
(Table <a href="#S4.T1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 1 ‣ 4.2.1 Datasets ‣ 4.2 Experimental Setup ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>), as well as the reasoning
quality of CoTs distilled under the ST setting
(Table <a href="#S4.T2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table 2 ‣ 4.3 Main Results ‣ 4 Experiments ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>). These results were obtained
by selecting the best-performing teacher as the representative. In this
section, we provide the detailed results for all individual teachers.
Specifically, Table <a href="#A2.T1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table A1 ‣ Appendix A2 Additional Results ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A1</span></a> presents the performance of
all teacher models across all tasks in both benchmarks, while
Table <a href="#A2.T2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table A2 ‣ Appendix A2 Additional Results ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A2</span></a> reports the quality
evaluation of the training CoT data distilled from each teacher LLM.

</div>

<div id="A2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

As presented in Table <a href="#A2.T1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table A1 ‣ Appendix A2 Additional Results ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A1</span></a>, the two most powerful
teacher models, Deepseek-R1 and Qwen3-235B-A22B-think, demonstrate a
significant advantage. The student models distilled from these teachers
outperform those from other teachers on the vast majority of scientific
tasks. This suggests that larger and more capable LLMs, with their
superior reasoning abilities, can generate more useful and
higher-quality data during the distillation process. Consequently, the
distilled CoTs exhibit more logical consistency and contain richer
knowledge. Furthermore, the results reported in
Table <a href="#A2.T2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Table A2 ‣ Appendix A2 Additional Results ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A2</span></a> confirm that these top-tier
reasoning LLMs generally achieve higher data utilization (*i.e*., pass
rate) and superior CoT quality. In contrast, other LLM thinkers that
rely on specific prompting strategies, such as Llama4-Scout-CoT, show a
clear gap in data utilization, indicating that reasoning LLMs may be
better at eliciting more comprehensive and complete reasoning.

</div>

<div id="A2.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

An interesting nuance can be observed between the top two teachers. On
BioProBench, DeepSeek-R1’s reasoning quality and win rate are slightly
lower than those of Qwen3-235B-A22B-think. We attribute this to
DeepSeek-R1 occasionally generating redundant steps, as tasks related to
experimental protocols prioritize the causal relationship between steps
over the exploration of multiple possibilities. Conversely, for tasks
requiring more fine-grained deliberation, such as molecule editing in
ChemCoTBench, DeepSeek-R1 showcases richer ideas and more rigorous
thinking, resulting in a higher overall quality score on that benchmark.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A3" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A3 </span>Implementation of Fine-tuning

<div id="A3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

In this work, to efficiently fine-tune LLMs, we leverage
LLaMA-Factory (Zheng et al.,
<a href="#bib.bib39" class="ltx_ref">2024</a>), DeepSpeed (Rasley
et al., <a href="#bib.bib25" class="ltx_ref">2020</a>) with ZeRO Stage
2 (Rajbhandari et al., <a href="#bib.bib24" class="ltx_ref">2020</a>),
and FlashAttention2 (Dao, <a href="#bib.bib7" class="ltx_ref">2023</a>)
across four NVIDIA A100 (80G) GPUs. We adopt AdamW (Loshchilov,
<a href="#bib.bib20" class="ltx_ref">2017</a>) as the optimizer with
$`\beta_{1}=0.9`$, $`\beta_{2}=0.95`$, and a weight decay of 0.1. The
peak learning rate is set to $`2\times 10^{-5}`$ with 10% warm-up steps
followed by cosine decay, a batch size of 32. We set the batch size to
32 and the maximum sequence length to 16,384. Training is conducted for
5 epochs to ensure optimal performance.

</div>

</div>

<div id="A4" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A4 </span>Details of CoT Quality Evaluation

<div id="A4.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

While the student models distilled with
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> achieve the
best overall performance, this does not
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">directly verify</span> the
quality of the data generated by
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>. To
comprehensively assess the quality of the synthesized CoTs, we adopt two
complementary evaluation strategies: 1)
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">absolute scoring</span> of
individual methods; and 2) <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">pairwise
comparison</span> between
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> and baselines.
These two strategies capture both absolute and relative perceptions of
CoT quality. The compared distillation baselines include
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Single Teacher</span> (ST),
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Multi Teacher</span> (MT), and
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Best-of-K</span> (BoK).

</div>

<div id="A4.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A4.1 </span>Absolute Scoring

<div id="A4.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Absolute scoring provides a quantitative measure of how CoTs perform
across dimensions of interest. Motivated by the design and objectives of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, we define the
following five dimensions for evaluating reasoning quality:

- <span id="A4.I1.i1"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A4.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Knowledge Diversity</span>:
  Whether the model recalls or introduces sufficient concepts, theorems,
  or knowledge fragments to support reasoning or verification.

  </div>
- <span id="A4.I1.i2"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A4.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Knowledge Accuracy</span>:
  Whether the knowledge retrieved or cited in reasoning is factually
  correct, so as to avoid propagating errors into the student model.

  </div>
- <span id="A4.I1.i3"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A4.I1.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Reasoning Diversity</span>:
  Whether the CoT explores meaningful alternative hypotheses or solution
  paths, promoting multi-perspective thinking and comprehensive
  understanding.

  </div>
- <span id="A4.I1.i4"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A4.I1.i4.p1" class="ltx_para">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Logical Coherence</span>: Whether
  the CoT follows a clear, consistent, and error-free logical
  progression, avoiding excessive leaps or confusion.

  </div>
- <span id="A4.I1.i5"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span></span>
  <div id="A4.I1.i5.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

  <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Step Redundancy</span>: Whether
  unnecessary or repetitive steps are minimized, yielding concise yet
  complete reasoning.

  </div>

</div>

<div id="A4.SS1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

For each CoT under evaluation, we employ GPT-5 as an LLM judge to assess
performance along the above five dimensions and assign an overall
integer score ranging from 0 to 10. The full prompt is provided in
Prompt <a href="#A4.SS1" class="ltx_ref"
title="A4.1 Absolute Scoring ‣ Appendix A4 Details of CoT Quality Evaluation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A4.1</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS1.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A4.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A4.2 </span>Pairwise Comparison

<div id="A4.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

While absolute scores quantify quality in isolation, they may not fully
capture relative improvements over baselines. To address this, we
conduct pairwise comparisons between CoTs distilled by
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> and those
generated by baseline methods.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

For fairness, only CoTs with correct answers that were selected for
training are included in the comparison. We again employ GPT-5 as the
LLM judge. Specifically, GPT-5 is given a pair of CoTs (one from
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, one from a
baseline) and asked to decide which trajectory is superior overall based
on the five evaluation dimensions. The prompt is detailed in
Prompt <a href="#A4.SS2" class="ltx_ref"
title="A4.2 Pairwise Comparison ‣ Appendix A4 Details of CoT Quality Evaluation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A4.2</span></a>. Importantly, GPT-5 is not
allowed to declare a tie, *i.e*., it must select the relatively better
CoT. We then report the win rate of each baseline as the proportion of
comparisons in which it was judged superior to
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.

</div>

<div id="A4.SS2.p3" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

id="A4.SS2.p3.pic1" class="ltx_picture" /></span>

</div>

<div id="A4.SS2.p4" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

The results of these two evaluation strategies are reported in Table 2
of Section 4.3. Since
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> is not compared
against itself, its win rate is omitted.
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> consistently
achieves higher absolute scores, surpassing 8.0 on average, reflecting
the robustness of its quality. Moreover, the baselines achieve win rates
below 40% in pairwise comparisons, confirming the significant relative
advantage of <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.

</div>

</div>

</div>

<div id="A5" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A5 </span>Prompts for <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>

<div id="A5.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This section details the prompts used in the evolutionary process of
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>, as described
in Section <a href="#S3" class="ltx_ref"
title="3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>. These prompts guide the LLMs
to perform specific evaluation, recombination, or mutation tasks on the
reasoning CoTs.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS1" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A5.1 </span>Fitness Function Prompts

<div id="A5.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

As described in Section <a href="#S3.SS2" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.2 Fitness Function ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.2</span></a>, to evaluate the quality of
candidate trajectories, we employ an LLM-as-a-Judge to assess the
knowledge usage correctness.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS1.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for knowledge usage correctness evaluation

<div id="A5.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This prompt instructs the LLM judge to score a given CoT based on its
application of the provided reference knowledge, returning a score from
1 to 5. The details of the prompt can be found in
Prompt <a href="#A5.SS1.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for knowledge usage correctness evaluation ‣ A5.1 Fitness Function Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.1</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A5.SS2" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A5.2 </span>CoT Recombination Prompts

<div id="A5.SS2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

The goal of recombination is to create superior offspring trajectories
that inherit and integrate the most effective strategies from different
parent CoTs, promoting the fusion of diverse thoughts and knowledge.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for Identifying the Binding Point

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This prompt tasks the recombiner model ($`C_{r}`$) with analyzing a
strategy provider CoT ($`t_{p}`$) to identify the endpoint of the last
reasonable thought, which serves as the crossover point. The specific
prompt is detailed in Prompt <a href="#A5.SS2.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for Identifying the Binding Point ‣ A5.2 CoT Recombination Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.2</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for Extracting Unique Information

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This prompt instructs the model to perform a comparative analysis
between the strategy provider CoT ($`t_{p}`$) and the target CoT
($`t_{o}`$). The goal is to isolate the unique, valuable reasoning steps
and knowledge that present only in the provider $`t_{p}`$. The specific
prompt is detailed in Prompt <a href="#A5.SS2.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for Extracting Unique Information ‣ A5.2 CoT Recombination Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.2</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for Generating a New Trajectory

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This is the final synthesis step of recombination. The prompt guides the
model to generate a new reasoning suffix, conditioned on the stable
prefix from the target CoT $`t_{o}`$ and the unique information
extracted from the provider $`t_{p}`$, thereby creating a novel, hybrid
CoT. The specific prompt is detailed in Prompt
<a href="#A5.SS2.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for Generating a New Trajectory ‣ A5.2 CoT Recombination Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.2</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS2.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A5.SS3" class="section ltx_subsection">

### <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">A5.3 </span>CoT Mutation Prompts

<div id="A5.SS3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

Mutation operators are designed for local optimization and error
correction. They introduce targeted variations into a single CoT to fix
flaws, enhance quality, and escape suboptimal reasoning patterns.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px1" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for Additive Mutation

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This prompt instructs the mutation model ($`\mathcal{M}_{u}`$) to enrich
a given CoT by elaborating on its reasoning. It is used to add more
logical details, deeper explanations, or relevant domain knowledge where
the original CoT was underdeveloped. The specific prompt is detailed in
Prompt <a href="#A5.SS3.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for Additive Mutation ‣ A5.3 CoT Mutation Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.3</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px1.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px2" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for Deletive Mutation

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

To improve the signal-to-noise ratio of the reasoning, this prompt
instructs the model to prune a given CoT. It removes redundant steps,
unproductive explorations, or extraneous information, resulting in a
more concise and efficient trajectory for student model training. The
specific prompt is detailed in Prompt
<a href="#A5.SS3.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for Deletive Mutation ‣ A5.3 CoT Mutation Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.3</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px3" class="section ltx_paragraph">

##### Prompt for Innovative Mutation

<div id="A5.SS3.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

This operator is a key mechanism for error correction in our framework.
The prompt guides the model to first diagnose the logical fallacies in a
faulty CoT by using the ground-truth answer as a reference, and to
summarize them into corrective advice. We then incorporate this advice
into the original query to generate a new, correct reasoning trajectory
that avoids the identified mistakes. The specific prompt is detailed in
Prompt <a href="#A5.SS3.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_ref"
title="Prompt for Innovative Mutation ‣ A5.3 CoT Mutation Prompts ‣ Appendix A5 Prompts for CoT-Evo ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">A5.3</span></a>.

</div>

<div id="A6" class="section ltx_appendix">

## <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_appendix">Appendix A6 </span>Transition Keywords

<div id="A6.p1" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

In <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span> framework,
particularly within the CoT Recombination described in Section
<a href="#S3.SS4" class="ltx_ref"
title="3.4 Reflective CoT Recombination and Mutation ‣ 3 CoT-Evo: Evolutionary CoT Distillation ‣ CoT-Evo: Evolutionary Distillation of Chain-of-Thought for Scientific Reasoning"><span
class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3.4</span></a>, we need a systematic way to
parse a continuous reasoning trajectory into a sequence of discrete,
coherent <span class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">thoughts</span>. To
achieve this, we utilize a predefined set of transition keywords. These
keywords act as linguistic markers that signal a shift, pause, or pivot
in the reasoning process. By identifying these keywords, our framework
can effectively segment a long CoT into meaningful, modular thoughts.
This segmentation is a critical prerequisite for identifying a suitable
"binding point", enabling the model to perform more structured and
meaningful recombination operations between different reasoning
trajectories. Below is the list of transition keywords employed in
<span class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CoT-Evo</span>.

</div>

<div id="A6.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">

<span class="ltx_inline-block"><img
src="data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iQTYucDIucGljMSIgY2xhc3M9Imx0eF9waWN0dXJlIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjQ1NjYuMDUiIG92ZXJmbG93PSJ2aXNpYmxlIiB2ZXJzaW9uPSIxLjEiIHZpZXdib3g9IjAgMCA2MDAgNDU2Ni4wNSIgd2lkdGg9IjYwMCI+PGcgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LXN0cm9rZS1jb2xvcjojMDAwMDAwOy0tbHR4LWZpbGwtY29sb3I6IzAwMDAwMDsiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0idHJhbnNsYXRlKDAsNDU2Ni4wNSkgbWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgMCkiIGZpbGw9IiMwMDAwMDAiIHN0cm9rZT0iIzAwMDAwMCIgc3Ryb2tlLXdpZHRoPSIwLjRwdCI+PGcgc3R5bGU9Ii0tbHR4LWZpbGwtY29sb3I6IzgwODBGRjsiIGZpbGw9IiM4MDgwRkYiIGZpbGwtb3BhY2l0eT0iMS4wIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0ic3Ryb2tlOm5vbmUiIGQ9Ik0gMCAxMi41IEwgMCA0NTUzLjU0IEMgMCA0NTYwLjQ1IDUuNiA0NTY2LjA1IDEyLjUgNDU2Ni4wNSBMIDU4Ny41IDQ1NjYuMDUgQyA1OTQuNCA0NTY2LjA1IDYwMCA0NTYwLjQ1IDYwMCA0NTUzLjU0IEwgNjAwIDEyLjUgQyA2MDAgNS42IDU5NC40IDAgNTg3LjUgMCBMIDEyLjUgMCBDIDUuNiAwIDAgNS42IDAgMTIuNSBaIiAvPjwvZz48ZyBzdHlsZT0iLS1sdHgtZmlsbC1jb2xvcjojRjdGN0ZGOyIgZmlsbD0iI0Y3RjdGRiIgZmlsbC1vcGFjaXR5PSIxLjAiPjxwYXRoIHN0eWxlPSJzdHJva2U6bm9uZSIgZD0iTSAwLjY5IDEyLjUgTCAwLjY5IDQ1MzguNTMgTCA1OTkuMzEgNDUzOC41MyBMIDU5OS4zMSAxMi41IEMgNTk5LjMxIDUuOTggNTk0LjAyIDAuNjkgNTg3LjUgMC42OSBMIDEyLjUgMC42OSBDIDUuOTggMC42OSAwLjY5IDUuOTggMC42OSAxMi41IFoiIC8+PC9nPjxnIGZpbGwtb3BhY2l0eT0iMS4wIiB0cmFuc2Zvcm09Im1hdHJpeCgxLjAgMC4wIDAuMCAxLjAgMjEuNDUgNDU0OC44MykiPjxmb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0IHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1mZy1jb2xvcjojRkZGRkZGOy0tZm9fd2lkdGggOjQwLjI2ZW07LS1mb19oZWlnaHQ6MC42OWVtOy0tZm9fZGVwdGggOjAuMTllbTsiIHdpZHRoPSI1NTcuMTEiIGhlaWdodD0iMTIuMyIgdHJhbnNmb3JtPSJtYXRyaXgoMSAwIDAgLTEgMCA5LjYxKSIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIGNvbG9yPSIjRkZGRkZGIj48c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3RfY29udGFpbmVyIj48c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3RfY29udGVudCI+CjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfaW5saW5lLWJsb2NrIGx0eF9taW5pcGFnZSBsdHhfYWxpZ25fYm90dG9tIiBzdHlsZT0id2lkdGg6MzUuMDFlbTsiPgo8c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X3AiPjxzcGFuIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfdGV4dCBsdHhfZm9udF9ib2xkIj5UcmFuc2l0aW9uIEtleXdvcmRzIGluIENvVDwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+Cjwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdD48L2c+PGcgZmlsbC1vcGFjaXR5PSIxLjAiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEuMCAwLjAgMC4wIDEuM
